For the redesign of the right-hand column, here's my design thinking:

The element's JOB: demonstrate the core value prop — "supervisor opens the exact turn, marks the decision point, assigns a retry; transcript is preserved." It should look like an actual app window / review interface, making the product tangible.

Design: "Supervisor review" panel styled like a real clinical record interface:
- **Window header**: functional app bar — case ID (mono chip), scenario title, status. Like a real tool, not decoration.
- **Transcript timeline**: vertical rail on the left (a line with time ticks) — turns hang off it. Client and trainee turns differentiated by speaker label + alignment. This reads as "record" not "chat bubble theater."
- **The supervisor mark**: the hero of the design — an annotation that visually CONNECTS to the exact turn, with the "A" marker on the rail, note text, and an "Assign retry" action chip. This is the product's differentiator, so make it the visual centerpiece.
- **Retry turn**: visibly tagged "DIRECTED RETRY" so the story completes: mark → instruction → retry.
- **Footer**: record status line.

Color treatment: keep it calm — paper background, one accent (ink) for the mark rail, maybe a single accent color for the supervisor annotation (the existing palette uses np tokens). Typography: mono for metadata (times, labels), body for dialogue.

Let me be concrete about CSS. I'll design with the existing np tokens (--np-bg, --np-surface, --np-line, etc.) which the empathy CSS already defines.

Layout specifics:
- `.empathy-console` — a bordered panel, generous radius (subtle, 8-12px), surface background, fine border, a very soft shadow for lift (ONE shadow, not decorative glow)
- Header: flex row, bottom hairline; left: case-id mono chip + title; right: status pill (outlined)
- Body: timeline with left rail — `border-left: 2px` on a container, turns with marker dots. Actually better: each turn has a grid: [rail-dot | content]. The rail is a continuous vertical line via the container's ::before or per-turn borders.
- Turns: speaker label (mono, uppercase, small) + message (body). Client vs trainee differentiated by dot color/label color.
- Review mark: breaks the rail with a distinct node — square/diamond marker with "A" inside, accent background, note below. Full-width annotation card with tinted background.
- Retry turn: same structure as trainee turn but with a small "DIRECTED RETRY" tag next to the speaker label.
- Footer: hairline top, mono status chips.

This gives it an "annotated record" feel — clinical, credible, and it tells the product story. That's a real redesign, not decoration-stripping.

Also — responsive: stack on mobile (grid → single column). The existing empathy-hero__grid presumably has a media query; I'll check.

**Console redesign** — making it look like a real supervisor-review interface rather than a "hollywood square":

The purpose per the user's earlier copy: "Open any attempt at the exact turn that mattered. Mark the decision point, leave a note, and assign a retry — the transcript is already there."

Design concept: **"Annotated record" — a transcript with a supervisor annotation pinned between turns.**

Key design moves:
1. **Speaker-alignment rhythm**: Client turns and trainee turns get different alignment/treatment so it reads as a dialogue record, not uniform stacked boxes. I'll differentiate via a left rail: each turn gets a marker on a continuous vertical rail line. The rail makes it feel like a document timeline.
2. **The supervisor mark as the centerpiece**: an annotation node ON the rail (letter A chip, ink background), with the note content set slightly inset, connected visually to the moment it marks. The note card gets a tinted treatment distinct from dialogue.
3. **Retry tagged**: the final trainee turn gets a "DIRECTED RETRY" mono tag next to the speaker label — completing the story arc: attempt → mark → retry.
4. **App-like header/footer**: case ID in mono chip, status pill, footer with record facts. Functional, not decorative.

Implementation approach on the existing classes (`.empathy-case`, `.empathy-transcript`, `.empathy-turn`, `.empathy-review`): I'll rework their CSS to the rail design. This is better than adding new classes since the responsive rules already reference them.

New CSS design (replacing lines 213-293):

```css
.empathy-transcript {
  position: relative;
  display: grid;
  gap: 0;
  list-style: none;
  margin: 0;
  padding: 1.25rem 1.5rem;
  border-left: 2px solid transparent; — hmm
}
```

Actually, let me think about the rail. A clean way: the transcript container gets `position: relative` and a `::before` vertical line at a fixed x-offset (e.g., `left: calc(1.5rem + 0.25rem)`). Each turn is a grid `[rail-space | content]`. Turn markers (dots) sit on the rail.

Simpler and robust: grid with two columns — `[marker-col | text-col]`. The continuous rail line: container ::before absolutely positioned running down through the marker column's center. Turn markers are the first grid item in each turn row.

Structure per turn:
```html
<li class="empathy-turn empathy-turn--client">
  <span class="empathy-turn__marker" aria-hidden="true"></span>
  <div class="empathy-turn__body">
    <span class="empathy-turn__who">Client · 18:42</span>
    <p class="empathy-turn__message">...</p>
  </div>
</li>
```

The marker column width: ~1.5rem, containing a dot (client = hollow/outline dot, trainee = solid ink dot). The rail: `.empathy-transcript::before { content:''; position:absolute; top/bottom; left: center-of-marker-col; width: 1px; background: var(--empathy-line-strong); }` — a hairline, with dots punched over it (dot background = page background ring + border... actually simpler: dots have background matching the case surface so they mask the rail where they sit, with a border for contrast).

The review node:
```html
<li class="empathy-review">
  <span class="empathy-review__marker">A</span>
  <div>
    <span class="empathy-review__who">Supervisor mark A</span>
    <strong>Defended intent before naming impact.</strong>
    <p>...</p>
  </div>
</li>
```
Marker "A" = ink square with white letter, sits on the rail, LARGER than turn dots → visual hierarchy: the supervisor mark is the biggest node on the timeline. Note card: subtle hover-tint background, maybe a left border connecting from the marker. I'll give the review a tinted panel + the marker. And an "assign retry" affordance — a small mono chip "→ RETRY ASSIGNED" in the review, which explains the arrow to the final turn.

Retry turn: trainee marker but with a small "RETRY" tag in the who-label. Tag: mono, outlined chip.

Also the client/turn differentiation: client messages get a slight indent style vs trainee? Actually cleaner: keep all left-aligned on the rail, differentiate only by marker + who-label color. The story reads down the rail. That's the "record" feel — chronological document, not chat theater. 

Font sizes: message text slightly larger for readability (--np-text-small exists; maybe keep). Who-labels: mono instead of label font for a "record metadata" feel? The existing uses --np-font-label uppercase. Mono for timestamps already. I'll use mono for who labels to strengthen the clinical-record identity: `Client · 18:42` in mono, small.

Header: case ID chip (mono, bordered), title, status pill (ink bg — keep existing). Add a subtle "supervisor view" bar? Actually the existing header is fine structurally; I'll keep `.empathy-case__head` mostly but make the ID a chip.

Footer: keep, mono small.

Panel container `.empathy-case`: keep the bordered flat panel (it matches the Swiss style), maybe add a very subtle shadow for lift: `box-shadow: 0 1px 0 rgba(...)`. Keep square corners — the design system is Swiss/editorial (square corners everywhere: border-right items, etc.). A gentle radius (4-6px) could soften... no — consistency with the rest of the page (everything square) wins. The "hollywood" feel came from stacked full-width alternating-bg boxes with heavy borders, not from radius. The rail redesign fixes the rhythm.

READTASTES.md 
Read 1/1 file, 162 lines
1: # TASTES.md
2: 
3: Aesthetic guidelines and design constraints for Pixelated Empathy.
4: 
5: > **Doctrine in effect:** Neutral Precision — "The Quiet Instrument." This file
6: > reflects what is actually shipping, not an aspirational brand. If you change
7: > the design system, change this file in the same PR.
8: 
9: ---
10: 
11: ## 1. Visual & Color System ("Neutral Precision")
12: 
13: Zero-chroma editorial system. All colors derive from the OKLCH grayscale ramp
14: defined in `src/styles/np-tokens.css`.
15: 
16: ### Surfaces
17: 
18: - **Bg Primary**: `oklch(0.1 0 0)` — deep space void (page canvas)
19: - **Bg Secondary / Surface**: `oklch(0.14 0 0)` — elevated cards, the
20:   transcript proof object, the contact form
21: - **Bg Elevated**: `oklch(0.18 0 0)` — supervisor annotation row in the
22:   transcript
23: - **Borders / dividers**: `rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.06)` on dark,
24:   `rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.08)` on light
25: 
26: ### Text
27: 
28: - **Text Primary**: `oklch(0.93 0 0)` (dark) / `oklch(0.15 0 0)` (light)
29: - **Text Secondary / Muted**: `oklch(0.55 0 0)` — labels, captions, the lede
30: - **Text Mid**: `oklch(0.7 0 0)` (dark) / `oklch(0.45 0 0)` (light)
31: 
32: ### Accent
33: 
34: There is no chromatic accent. **No orange, no blue, no green.** The chromatic
35: guardrail in `np-tokens.css` (`--np-selection`, the zero-chroma rule block)
36: enforces this at the token level. The legacy alias `--accent-primary` exists
37: in `np-tokens.css` solely to absorb marketing.css's variable references; it
38: resolves to `var(--np-text)` (light gray on dark), not to any colored value.
39: 
40: If a clinical surface genuinely needs a semantic accent (e.g. a crisis /
41: risk signal), the decision must be made explicitly in a separate pass and
42: tokenized before shipping.
43: 
44: ---
45: 
46: ## 2. Typography
47: 
48: Three families, three jobs. No fourth family without a real contrast reason.
49: 
50: - **Display / Heading**: Instrument Serif — hero display, major section
51:   headings. Weight 400. Tracking `-0.035em`. `text-wrap: balance` on hero
52:   and section titles.
53: - **Body / Paragraph**: Instrument Sans Variable — ledes, card copy,
54:   transcript dialog. Weight 400. Lede line-height `1.55` (short-form bridge
55:   spec). Long-form body uses `1.65`. `text-wrap: pretty` on ledes.
56: - **UI / Badge**: Spline Sans Mono Variable — eyebrows, labels, buttons,
57:   timestamps, code blocks, supervisor annotations. Weight 500. All caps with
58:   `0.04em–0.16em` tracking.
59: 
60: Measure: 60–68ch on ledes and cards. Hero h1 clamped `2.125–3.25rem` with a
61: max measure of 16ch. Medium h2 clamped `1.5–2rem` with a max measure of 22ch.
62: 
63: ---
64: 
65: ## 3. Structural Constraints (Technical Brutalism)
66: 
67: - **Flat Over Round**: Strict minimal border-radius. Default `0px` everywhere.
68:   Pill shapes, rounded buttons, and gradient blobs are out of scope.
69: - **No Heavy Shadows**: Never soft diffuse box-shadows for elevation. Rely
70:   on 1px border outlines, surface ramps (`--np-bg` → `--np-surface` →
71:   `--np-elevated`), or solid offset shadows.
72: - **Grid Exposure**: Page-level grids may use thin 1px `--np-line` borders,
73:   dotted backgrounds (`radial-gradient`), or hairline rules as visual texture.
74:   Hero panels use a subtle 2.75rem grid mask fading to transparent.
75: - **Component Shape**: Cards are content-shaped (discrete, scannable).
76:   One card inside another is never right; flatten with type and dividers
77:   instead. Wrappers that exist only to add margin should be deleted.
78: 
79: ---
80: 
81: ## 4. Interaction & Motion
82: 
83: - **Hover states**: 150ms `var(--np-ease)`. On cards and CTAs, a `1px`
84:   background lift (`var(--np-surface)`) and a `translateY(-1px)` on the
85:   primary button. No permanent elevation on any non-hovered control.
86: - **Focus rings**: 2px solid `var(--np-mid)` with 3px offset, on **all**
87:   interactive surfaces — nav, theme toggle, form fields, buttons, marketing
88:   CTAs. This is a hard constraint, not a default. 1px focus rings are a
89:   regression to fix.
90: - **Touch targets**: Minimum 44×44px (48×48px comfortable). The visual element
91:   can be smaller; the hit area must be 44px. The shared header toggle is 44×44.
92: - **Reduced motion**: Every animated surface must include a
93:   `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block that drops transition
94:   duration to `0.01ms`. Do not ship motion without the fallback.
95: - **Form fields**: ≥16px font-size on inputs/textareas under 640px to prevent
96:   iOS Safari auto-zoom on focus. `font-size: 1rem` minimum.
97: 
98: ---
99: 
100: ## 5. Stylesheet Layout
101: 
102: Three CSS files, three jobs. Do not collapse them.
103: 
104: - **`src/styles/np-tokens.css`** — Token source of truth: colors, type, space,
105:   radii, motion, z-index, light/dark theme. The `:root` and `[data-theme=light]`
106:   blocks. Legacy compatibility aliases for the marketing theme
107:   (`--accent-primary`, `--accent-primary-rgb`, `--font-body`, `--ease-out`).
108: - **`src/styles/np-landing.css`** — Homepage (`/`) styles. Loaded only by
109:   `src/pages/index.astro`. Defines `.np-page`, `.np-hero`, `.np-hero__transcript`,
110:   the proof-object grid, the CTA band, the help-link footer.
111: - **`src/styles/marketing.css`** — The 7 marketing pages (`/features`, `/trust`,
112:   `/about`, `/press`, `/team`, `/company`, `/contact`). Loaded by
113:   `src/layouts/BaseLayout.astro`. Defines `.marketing-hero`, `.marketing-title`,
114:   `.marketing-input`, `.marketing-button`, `.marketing-cta-band`, etc.
115: - **`src/styles/fm-fusion.css`** — Self-contained FM hairline-grid layer
116:   used by `FmHairlineGrid`, `FmChip`, `FmEyebrow` components on `/features`.
117:   Loaded by `BaseLayout.astro` alongside `marketing.css`.
118: 
119: **Dead code (do not delete without sign-off):**
120: 
121: - `src/styles/homepage.css` — decorative system with radial gradients and
122:   `backdrop-filter: blur`. Has zero importers as of the most recent audit.
123:   Imported by some compiled outputs only. Documented here so future
124:   contributors do not re-import it.
125: - `src/styles/variables.css` — orange-accent, light-first legacy. Not
126:   imported by any marketing page; importing it globally would override the
127:   NP zero-chroma rule on `html { background-color } !important`. Keep as a
128:   reference, do not import.
129: 
130: **Do not import `variables.css` from `BaseLayout.astro`.** It uses
131: `!important` on `html { background-color }` and `body { background-color }`
132: and a global link underline animation, which would override the NP chrome.
133: 
134: ---
135: 
136: ## 6. Page-Surface Rules
137: 
138: - **Homepage** (`/`): brand register. Literata display 68px hero, transcript
139:   proof object on the right (Session 04 · Rupture Repair), single dominant
140:   action, help-link footer with 4 destination cards.
141: - **Marketing pages**: brand register with a 2-tier scale. Display h1 at
142:   57.6px, medium h2 at 22.4–34.4px, lede 16px. Each page carries a
143:   per-page proof object in the hero (4 capability tracks for /features,
144:   3 trust topics for /trust, founder card for /team, etc.) — not the
145:   generic 3-up stats grid that was there before.
146: - **Contact** (`/contact`): Configure register. Hero proof object previews
147:   the form (what to bring); the form is below in a `marketing-card` with
148:   visible 1px borders, 16px font, 2px focus ring.
149: 
150: ---
151: 
152: ## 7. Drift to Refuse
153: 
154: - Anything orange, blue, green, or purple on a public surface — unless an
155:   explicit accent decision has been made and tokenized.
156: - Gradient hero blobs, glassmorphism, pill buttons, centered-icon-card rows.
157: - Lorem ipsum, "Welcome to [Product]" copy, exclamation points.
158: - Display fonts in product labels. Mono in display headlines.
159: - "AI-y" decorative motifs (cyan gradient rings, animated cursor arrows).
160: - The "Dark Metal" orange doctrine from the prior TASTES.md — superseded.
161:   Do not reintroduce `#ff8533` or any other chromatic accent without a
162:   deliberate product decision.

#!/usr/bin/env node
/**
 * Build wrapper that handles EPIPE errors gracefully
 *
 * Problem: During CI builds, parent process may close stdout/stderr pipes
 * before the build completes, causing Node.js to throw unhandled EPIPE errors
 *
 * Solution: This wrapper handles broken pipe errors and exits cleanly
 */
import { spawn, spawnSync } from 'child_process'
import fs from 'fs'
import path from 'path'
import process from 'process'

import dotenv from 'dotenv'

// Remove stale build output before every build.
// This avoids rare runtime mismatches where the manifest references hashed assets
// that were removed/renamed in a previous build output (for example, 500*.mjs chunks).
const distDir = path.resolve(process.cwd(), 'dist')
if (fs.existsSync(distDir)) {
  fs.rmSync(distDir, { recursive: true, force: true })
}

const verifyCommand = ['node', 'scripts/ci/verify-build-contract.mjs']
const verify = spawnSync(verifyCommand[0], verifyCommand.slice(1), {
  stdio: 'inherit',
})
if (verify.status !== 0) {
  process.exit(verify.status ?? 1)
}

// Try to load .env file manually to ensure variables are available
try {
  const envPath = path.resolve(process.cwd(), '.env')
  if (fs.existsSync(envPath)) {
    const result = dotenv.config({ path: envPath })
    if (result.error) {
      console.warn(
        '⚠️  Warning: Failed to load .env file via dotenv:',
        result.error.message,
      )
    }
  }
} catch (e) {
  console.warn('⚠️  Warning: Error trying to load .env file:', e.message)
}

// Handle EPIPE errors on stdout/stderr
process.stdout.on('error', (error) => {
  if (error.code === 'EPIPE') {
    // Parent process closed the pipe - exit gracefully
    // This is not a fatal error, just means logs won't be printed
    process.exit(0)
  }
  throw error
})

process.stderr.on('error', (error) => {
  if (error.code === 'EPIPE') {
